Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

- [ ] This PR targets `develop` and is rebased onto the latest `origin/develop`
      with zero conflicts (`git fetch origin && git rebase origin/develop`).
- [ ] `bun install` and `bun run verify` were run after sync, or any failure is
      recorded below with the exact unrelated blocker.
- [ ] A reviewer can confirm the change works without reading the code, from the
      evidence attached below.

# Contribution provenance

Declare the actual assistance used for implementation and review. This is
self-reported provenance, not a request for chain-of-thought. Never include
prompts containing secrets, tokens, private session IDs, or hidden reasoning.
Use exact `provider/model-id` values; `GPT`, `Claude`, or `AI` are not specific
enough. Human-only work must say so explicitly.

The row labels below must **not** reuse the terminal eliza.army footer labels
(`Client / agent tooling`, `Skill revision` / `Contribution skill revision`,
`Attribution status`). Duplicating those strings makes the scoring validator
reject the machine marker (#17855). Keep the `<!-- attribution-row:* -->`
markers; only the human-readable prefixes changed. After filling these rows,
append the standard footer once at the end of the PR body (do not repeat the
visible footer lines here).

# Evidence Gate

Evidence must match the exact commit reviewed. `scripts/pr-evidence.mjs rows`
sets this marker from the live PR head; rerun it after every push.
<!-- evidence-head:replace-with-current-40-character-head-sha -->

Any change testable on the frontend is not mergeable without a video walkthrough,
before/after screenshots, and logs. If you did not attach them, say why.

Attach each applicable artifact **inline in this PR** (drag-and-drop into the
description or a comment), or write `N/A - <reason>` on the row. Do not leave
evidence rows blank. Videos must be **MP4** (GitHub renders them inline);
prefer **JPG over PNG** for screenshots. Do not commit evidence files to the
repo.

<!-- evidence-row:before-screenshots -->
- [ ] Before full-page screenshots are attached for every affected UI surface
      (desktop and mobile), or marked `N/A - <reason>`.
<!-- evidence-row:after-screenshots -->
- [ ] After full-page screenshots are attached for every affected UI surface
      (desktop and mobile), or marked `N/A - <reason>`.
<!-- evidence-row:walkthrough-video -->
- [ ] A video walkthrough of the complete user flow is attached, or marked
      `N/A - <reason>`.
<!-- evidence-row:backend-logs -->

      `N/A - <reason>`.
<!-- evidence-row:frontend-logs -->
- [ ] Frontend console and network logs show the request/response and state
      change, or are marked `N/A - <reason>`.
<!-- evidence-row:llm-trajectory -->
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectory is attached for agent/action/provider/prompt/model
      changes, or marked `N/A - <reason>`.
<!-- evidence-row:domain-artifacts -->
- [ ] Domain artifacts are attached where applicable (DB rows, memories,
      scheduled tasks, wallet/on-chain output, generated files, audio, etc.), or
      marked `N/A - <reason>`.
<!-- evidence-row:ocr-review -->
- [ ] OCR visual-text review output is attached for UI changes, or marked
      `N/A - <reason>` when the change has no rendered visual surface.

# Evidence Details

## Real LLM-call trajectory

For agent/action/provider/prompt/model changes, use a real live-model run, not
the deterministic proxy. Produce with:

```bash
  packages/scenario-runner/bin/eliza-scenarios run <scenario> --report <out.json>
```

Link the JSON report, run viewer, native jsonl, or write `N/A - <reason>`.

## Backend + frontend logs

Backend: structured logger lines ([ClassName] …) showing the code path firing end to end.
Frontend: console + network trace showing the request/response and state change.
Paste here inline (wrap long output in a `<details>` block), or write
`N/A - <reason>`.

## Screenshots (before / after) + video walkthrough

Full-page before AND after screenshots are required for any UI change. Include a
video click-through of the flow.

```bash
  bun run evidence:doctor                 (check capture tools; prints fixes for any missing)
  bun run test:e2e:record                 (general E2E recordings)
  bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app    (app + cloud UI — REQUIRED for UI changes)
```

A UI-touching diff (rendered `.tsx`/`.css`/`.svg` under `packages/app`,
`packages/ui`, `apps/app`, …) MUST attach real screenshot/video/OCR artifacts —
the CI evidence gate rejects `N/A` on those rows for such diffs, label or not.
If a capture tool is missing, `evidence:doctor` prints the install command;
install it rather than marking evidence `N/A`.

### After

### Walkthrough video

Or write `N/A - <reason>`.

## Audio / voice walkthrough

For voice / transcript / TTS / STT / omnivoice changes, attach captured audio of
the real round-trip plus a narrated walkthrough. Or write `N/A - <reason>`.

## Known gaps / failures

List any command failure, missing artifact, unavailable device, unavailable live
service, or evidence row marked N/A. Include the exact reason and why it is not a
blocker for this PR.

15:Tf2b,## Summary

Promotes the verified `develop` tip `def129e2ebdb33abe40fc7f17dadfb85cb8f4194` to production `main`.

This promotion includes #18886, which restores the complete iMessage onboarding continuation: legacy `eliza.app` links retain the onboarding session, login returns to `/get-started`, the authenticated user confirms and links the originating phone identity, and success offers a native **Back to iMessage** `sms:` deep link.

## Verification

- [x] constituent PR #18886 merged into `develop`
- [x] frozen-install regression repaired by #18993; `bun install --frozen-lockfile --no-save` passes
- [x] `bun install` on the rebased feature revision
- [x] `bun run verify` — 350/350 workspace build/typecheck tasks and repository audits passed
- [x] focused onboarding/backend/auth/gateway contracts — 161 tests passed
- [x] strict onboarding browser audit — 6/6 passed, 0 broken, 0 needs-work
- [x] hosted Pages preview preserves `onboardingSession` through `/login?returnTo=/get-started`
- [x] exact native return target asserted as `sms:+18087881821`
- [ ] post-deploy production iMessage → login → Messages return proof (performed after this promotion deploys)

## Risk and rollback

This is the repository's standard `develop` → `main` production promotion. The iMessage flow spans the Pages app, Cloud API, and gateway webhook service. If production verification fails, revert the promotion merge and redeploy the last known-good main revision.

## Evidence

Feature-level evidence is carried from #18886; deployment proof will be added after the production workflows finish.

## Rollout note

Staging deployment is intentionally gated after merge. The `eliza.app` Cloudflare zone is currently on the Free plan, whose Universal SSL certificate does not cover nested per-agent hosts such as `*.cloud-staging.eliza.app` or `*.cloud.eliza.app`. The Terraform and workflows model the required Advanced Certificate Manager packs, but the zone entitlement must be purchased/approved before those wildcard certificates can converge. # Background

## What does this PR do?

This applies the root-cause repairs produced by a second independent review of every open NubsCarson PR and the later #18750 mega-train:

- bounds and sanitizes planner synthesis and attachment enrichment, preserves native message stacking, accounts for model usage, and suppresses replay of ambiguous non-retryable substeps;
- sends ACP cancellation as a notification and correlates the original prompt terminal response, accepts documented acpx terminal frames without synthetic session IDs, and requires authoritative label readback receipts;
- cancels and compensates abandoned cloud joins, serializes node provisioning behind provider/environment quota and budget authority, preserves agent management when balance reads fail, and adds authenticated realtime canary rollback;
- clears notification inbox state on credential replacement and preserves socket authority boundaries;
- centralizes provider-owned endpoint resolution while keeping mock endpoints process-owned;
- closes Smithers/Effect/React compatibility boundaries and fails unsupported PGlite configurations closed;
- restores executable release safeguards, unifies desktop Vite source loading, preserves unprivileged Pages PR builds, and audits final iOS cloud artifacts;
- sanitizes terminal and coding-shell output across chat, attachments, background sessions, and history (including configured secrets), normalizes planner-only calendar selectors, and deterministically routes explicit owner reminders without hijacking calendar appointments.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes and safety improvements. No intentional breaking public API change.

# Risks

**Medium.** This touches macOS permission probing, Electrobun signing for dev/direct/MAS/updater/template builds, and the shared permission UI. The main risks are assigning the app identity too broadly, regressing Windows/Linux notification fallback, or confusing native errors with user denial. The implementation restricts identity rewriting to the top-level `Contents/MacOS/bun`, verifies it in every shipping path, retains renderer authority off native macOS, and has focused cross-platform tests.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The actual macOS notification caller is Electrobun's packaged Bun process. It was signed as `bun` while requesting authorization for `ai.elizaos.app`, so `usernotificationsd` rejected registration before a valid prompt and the UI reported a false denial.

This PR:

- signs and verifies the actual top-level Bun permission host as the app identity across local dev, direct release, MAS, updater, and generated-project paths without adding Apple-private entitlements;
- uses stable designated requirements and the canonical `com.apple.security.device.audio-input` entitlement;
- distinguishes native request/configuration errors from real user denial;
- routes Open Settings through native RPC, checks the exact permission freshly after returning, and renders request/recheck failures explicitly;
- preserves browser notification fallback on Windows/Linux and non-Electrobun macOS;
- fixes the one-line current-develop cold-launch blocker by using Electrobun's declared local logger in the shell sync relay.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No end-user documentation change is required. The project scaffold and package contracts are updated so newly generated desktop apps inherit the fixed signing/entitlement behavior.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Open the attached before/after artifacts.
2. Inspect the backend proof showing the prior native rejection, current bundle identity, `GRANTED`, and real scheduled notification.
3. Review the focused signing, permission, UI, and cross-platform tests.

## Detailed testing steps

- Build/run the macOS Electrobun app from this branch.

- Verify the Eliza row changes to `GRANTED` without a stale cache.
- Choose Desktop → Send Test Notification; verify the signed Bun host reports successful scheduling.
- Verify the app reaches healthy runtime state with zero hard-red renderer/runtime patterns.

# Risks

**Medium — production deploy topology only.** Dropping the shared workflow-level production group without a post-approval mutate lock would allow overlapping production mutations. This PR does not do that.

- Admission is per `run_id` (PRs still share `pr-{number}` and cancel in progress).
- `authorize-production` waits on `environment: production` with **no** mutation lock.
- `release` takes one `cloud-cf-release-v6-${env}` group (`cancel-in-progress: false`, `queue: max`) and `uses` `.github/workflows/cloud-cf-release.yml` for migrate + API + Pages + routing proof. That callee has no workflow-level or per-leg concurrency group.
- AASA CDN `--apple-cdn-live` stays inside the publish job. Provisioning SSH stays one locked job.

No Worker, Pages, Hetzner, secret, or janitor behavior changes beyond carrying #19128's Telegram bot pins into the extracted Pages build. A leftover `cloud-cf-deploy-v4/v5` waiter is orphaned by the `v6` suffix and can be cancelled by an operator.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Production acquired the shared workflow concurrency group **before any job exists**, so a waiting `environment: production` holder blocked newer canonical `main` at workflow `pending` / zero jobs (#18092). Staging already admitted before taking a shared lock.

This copies that topology and implements Shaw's review of the first head:

1. **Non-PR admission is per run, not per SHA.** Two dispatches/reruns of the same commit get distinct groups and both create jobs. PRs still supersede by number.
2. **One post-approval critical section.** `authorize-production` does not share a mutation group. `release` holds one lock across migrate, API, Pages, and routing proof. Run B cannot migrate while run A deploys API.
3. **Official GitHub queue contract.** `cancel-in-progress: false` is not a FIFO queue. Mutation locks set `queue: max` so additional approved releases wait (up to 100) instead of the default one-pending eviction. FIFO is by wait-start, not dispatch; ordering is not guaranteed. `queue: max` is not combined with `cancel-in-progress: true` (workflow validation error).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (CI / deploy admission topology). No product runtime change.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The reasoning lives in the workflow comments and the contract test.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Workflow-level `concurrency` in `cloud-cf-deploy.yml` (per-`run_id` / PR supersession).
2. `authorize-production` (environment only) vs `release` (`queue: max`, reusable workflow).
3. `packages/cloud/scripts/production-deploy-admission-lock.test.mjs` (state model, not `group:` string search).

# Risks

Medium. This changes public checkout navigation, provider deadlines, webhook ordering, scheduled expiry, and every payment lifecycle transition. Risk is bounded by conditional transactional repository transitions, a generic update API that cannot write lifecycle/terminal/event fields, real PGlite interleaving tests, authenticated cron wiring, failure-sensitive provider/webhook tests, and a joined public-route-to-hosted-page contract test.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Makes initialize, fail, cancel, settle, and expire explicit transactional state transitions with allowed-state/deadline guards and durable events emitted from the returned row. Concurrent terminal transitions cannot produce mixed status/settlement payloads.
- Preserves deliberate same-transition idempotency while rejecting conflicting replays with the current state, including initialization payload conflicts.
- Deletes the generic status-writing path: ordinary payment-request updates are structurally limited to mutable intent fields.
- Fails closed for overdue public requests, expires them from an authenticated scheduled route, and binds Stripe/OxaPay checkout lifetimes to the application deadline.
- Persists Stripe webhook settlement before dedupe/publication so failed or late callbacks retry safely and cannot credit an expired request.
- Isolates payment-page route generations, tracks the live deadline, revalidates authoritative state immediately before redirect, and renders provider labels instead of storage enum strings.
- Verifies the anonymous public contract across real auth middleware, detail route, service, repository, and PGlite. The exact eight-field serialized fixture returned there is consumed by a mounted `PaymentRequestPage` test, joining the DTO-to-hosted-page seam without claiming a live-deployment browser E2E.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking payment-integrity and checkout-navigation repair).

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change is required. The behavior is enforced in code, tests, scheduled configuration, and the generated Cloud router.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with the payment repository lifecycle-transition methods and PGlite interleavings, then the payment service replay tests and unified webhook/cron routes, and finally the public-response integration test plus `PaymentRequestPage` contract test.

# Sync with develop

- [x] `bun run verify` passes post-sync: 350/350 Turbo tasks plus all repository audits.
- [x] Preserved and validated Shaw's stable-identity follow-up commit after sync.

# Risks

Medium. This adds a post-settlement `ACTION_COMPLETED` observer and durable engagement writes. Attribution is deliberately restricted to exact stable identities, successful applied receipts, recent rendered items, owner occurrence completion, and direct calendar time changes. Failures remain visible through `runtime.reportError` without rewriting the already-settled owner action.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Persists only surfaced morning-brief items as immutable engagement events.
- Attributes owner occurrence completions and directly applied calendar reschedules to recently rendered items by stable source identity.
- Closes expired impression windows deterministically as ignored, while preserving positive engagement.
- Adds owner-only `BRIEF recalibrate` and `BRIEF reset_recalibration` operations with durable/no-op effect receipts.
- Makes reset reversible: later evidence can relearn and demote a restored class again.
- Leaves the optimizer and PR #18584's summary-loader slice unchanged.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature — non-breaking LifeOps morning-brief engagement and owner control.

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is required. The public action schema, descriptions, receipts, and tests document the new operations.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with `engagement.pglite.integration.test.ts` for the durable contract and `brief-action.test.ts` for the owner-facing recalibrate/reset behavior.

bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-personal-assistant lint
bun run verify

# Risks

Low. Opt-in plugin (`no autoEnable`), listed in `UNBUNDLED_OPTIONAL_PLUGINS` with
`skipOnMobile: true`, so it is not baked into the mobile APK. Characters that do
not list `@elizaos/plugin-companion` are unchanged. Connecting requires an
explicit `COMPANION_PAIRING_TOKEN`; commands are rejected until a
`welcome`/`register` handshake records a `deviceId`.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds `@elizaos/plugin-companion`: a desktop/node WebSocket **client** so an
agent can drive an ESP32 companion device that already hosts the JSON protocol
on SoftAP (`SET_MOOD`, `GET_STATUS`, touch events). Firmware is out of tree;
this package speaks that protocol and is covered by an in-process WebSocket
stand-in so CI never needs hardware.

## What kind of change is this?

Features (non-breaking change which adds functionality)

# Documentation changes needed?

Plugin-local `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and `CLAUDE.md` are included. No app or
docs-site change.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-companion/src/__tests__/service.mock-device.test.ts` and
`auth.test.ts`. Then `src/companion-client.ts` and `src/protocol.ts`.

## Detailed testing steps

Automated tests are the review path:

```bash
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-companion test
```

P0 use cases covered: handshake, SET_MOOD, GET_STATUS, touch provider, pairing
token 401, command-before-handshake, invalid mood, unknown-command, malformed
JSON, pong timeout / disconnect, missing `deviceId`.

25:Tded,+Fixes #16342 by making Cloud API webhook routing account-scoped and inbound processing durably idempotent.

## What changed

- Unknown or missing `metadata.phone_number_id` fails closed whenever Cloud API accounts are configured.
- Duplicate Cloud API phone-number IDs and duplicate normalized account IDs fail startup.
- Named accounts inherit policy defaults only; provider identity, transport, and credentials never leak from the default/base/env account.
- Each inbound delivery uses a distinct deterministic claim document, separate from the real inbound message ID.
- Initial acquisition uses the store's unique insert. Reclaim and terminal transitions use the adapter's atomic document compare-and-swap revision contract.
- Random lease UUIDs fence stale owners; late zombie completion fails with a typed conflict.
- Missing claim storage fails closed before connection, room, model, or Meta side effects.
- Completion/failure transition errors are propagated and reported; none are swallowed.
- If a host persisted the real message but died before completing its claim, redelivery converges on the persisted message without replaying side effects.

# Risks

**Low–medium.** All changes are inside the cloud wake/provision wait in the typed client.

- Failures that previously polled silently until the six-minute timeout now throw immediately as `CloudAgentWakeError`, including HTTP failures and resolved `{ success: false }` envelopes from resume, agent-detail reads, and job reads. Only status-less transport failures and explicit 500/502/504 infrastructure churn remain transient inside the bounded poll. Control-plane codes and `Retry-After` metadata survive both browser and native transports.
- A fresh 202 create that reports a `jobId` now polls `/api/v1/jobs/:jobId` to terminal before the agent-detail wait. A backend that answers with a jobId but no job read endpoint would previously have been ignored; a transient job-read failure only ticks, and a create with no `jobId` (warm pool / sync create) is byte-identical to before.
- `cause` is preserved on every typed error, so the join flow's `isCloudAgentGoneError` stale-binding recovery (404 + `agent_not_found`) continues to classify through the wrapper.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The dedicated-agent wake wait (`waitForCloudAgentRunning`) swallowed every resume and status-read failure (`.catch(() => null)`), so auth expiry (401), credit exhaustion (402), a deleted agent row (404), lifecycle conflicts (409), and worker/capacity outages (503) all rendered as the same "Starting your agent…" spinner until a generic six-minute timeout. The fresh-create path also received the backend's provisioning `jobId` on the 202 response and hardcoded it away (`jobId: ""`), reducing a failed provision to that same opaque timeout instead of the job row's recorded failure reason.

This PR repairs the state machine at the API-client layer:

- **`CloudAgentWakeError`** — typed error with `phase` (`resume` / `status-poll` / `provision-job` / `failed` / `timeout`), the HTTP `status` of the underlying control-plane failure, `retryAfter` (seconds, from the backend's Retry-After header or error body) when sent, `lastObservedStatus`, and the `agentId`/`jobId` operator-safe correlation ids. Timeout messages now name the last truthful status, elapsed time, and the agent id — never a fabricated healthy state.
- **Terminal failures surface immediately** from resume, detail, and job operations, whether the adapter rejects or resolves a `{ success: false }` envelope. Status-less network errors and explicit 500/502/504 responses remain transient; every other HTTP status is returned to the join boundary with preserved control-plane code and `Retry-After` metadata.
- **The fresh-create `jobId` is preserved** through `parseDirectCloudAgentCreateData` and `createCloudCompatAgent`, and `selectOrProvisionCloudAgent` follows the canonical job (`getCloudCompatJobStatus`, `/api/v1/jobs/:jobId`) to a terminal state via the new exported `waitForCloudProvisionJob` before binding — a failed provision surfaces the job's real reason (e.g. "no provisioning worker available") within one poll interval instead of six minutes of agent-detail polling.

Issue acceptance coverage (client-state-machine items): typed phase/source exposure → public `CloudAgentWakeError.phase` + preserved `onProgress` vocabulary; jobId preserved and polled to terminal → `waitForCloudProvisionJob`; no swallowed rejected or resolved-failure resume/detail/job operations, with retry only for explicitly transient states → classifier + tests; truthful timeout with correlation id → timeout message carries last status, elapsed seconds, agent/job ids. The rendered-UI phase display, elapsed-time UI, safe sign-out escape, and real-staging E2E rows belong to the join page surface and gauss's acceptance lane and are **not** claimed here (see Known gaps).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

## Review follow-ups (second pass)

Three defects found in review of the first pass are fixed here:

1. **One deadline for the whole join.** `waitForCloudProvisionJob` and the `waitForCloudAgentRunning` that follows it each received the full `CLOUD_AGENT_WAKE_TIMEOUT_MS`, so a job completing at 5:59 handed a fresh six minutes to the status poll — up to twelve minutes, which is the literal complaint in #18463. `selectOrProvisionCloudAgent` now computes one deadline and passes the remaining budget to each wait.
2. **408/429 are transient, and Retry-After is slept, not thrown.** `CLOUD_WAKE_TRANSIENT_STATUSES` treated every status outside 500/502/504 as terminal, so a single rate-limited or timed-out poll tick aborted an otherwise healthy join. 408 and 429 are now transient, and the `Retry-After` the classifier already extracted paces the next tick instead of decorating a thrown error.
3. **The real `ElizaError`, not a guarded base.** `const WakeErrorBase = (ElizaError ?? Error)` was production code bending around a broken test stub: in the degraded mode `code`/`context` are dropped, so `isCloudAgentGoneError` returned false in fixture-e2e and true in production — the fixtures exercised a different class than users get. The `@elizaos/core` esbuild stub the `__e2e__` runners share now exports a real `ElizaError`, and `CloudAgentWakeError extends ElizaError` directly.

New coverage:

- `client-cloud-wake-typed-errors.test.ts` — `CloudAgentWakeError` is an `ElizaError` carrying `code` + `context`; a 429 and a 408 status read keep polling to a running agent; the loop waits the ~120ms `Retry-After` the control plane named (agent poll and job poll); and the provisioning wait plus the running wait finish inside ONE `wakeTimeoutMs` rather than two.
- `packages/ui/src/testing/e2e-runner/esbuild-stubs.test.ts` — bundles a module that subclasses `ElizaError` through real esbuild with `stubElizaCore()` and evaluates the output, asserting `instanceof`, `code`, `context`, and `cause` survive, while unnamed core symbols still resolve to the no-op proxy and core's Node graph is never bundled.

```
$ cd packages/core && node ../shared/scripts/generate-keywords.mjs

# Risks

Low-medium. The change is strictly additive: a third `mode="simulate"` alongside `prepare`/`execute`, an optional `simulate`/`simulation` member on `WalletChainHandler`, and a widened `WalletRouterExecution.status` union. `prepare`/`dryRun` semantics are byte-for-byte unchanged (the pre-existing test locking that behavior is untouched and green). The riskiest surface — anything that signs or broadcasts — is exactly what simulation is proven never to touch: test spies throw on `sendTransaction`/`sendRawTransaction`/`confirmTransaction` and on `VersionedTransaction.prototype.sign`, and the simulate paths resolve only the wallet public key. The financial-confirmation gate and the post-confirmation mode-forcing share one predicate (`requiresWalletFinancialConfirmation`), so a simulate request can never be upgraded to `execute`.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Implements the typed, non-broadcasting Solana simulation mode #16613 asks for, replacing fabricated dry-run metadata with a real RPC simulation for Jupiter swaps and pump.fun buys:

- **Before:** `WalletBackendService.routeWalletAction` returned a fabricated `status:"prepared"` object built from static per-handler capability metadata for every non-bridge `prepare`/`dryRun` request — no chain handler call, no transaction, no RPC, nothing a user could trust as a preview (the defect pinned by the 2026-07-23 triage comment on the issue).
- **After:** `mode="simulate"` builds the **exact same real, unsigned transaction the execute path would sign and send** — the Jupiter quote + swap-tx build and the PumpPortal trade-local build are factored into shared helpers (`fetchJupiterSwapTransaction`, `fetchPumpFunTransaction`) used verbatim by both execute and simulate, so simulation can never drift from what execute submits — and runs it through `connection.simulateTransaction({ sigVerify: false, replaceRecentBlockhash: true })`, returning a typed `WalletRouterSimulation` (`success`, `err`, `logs`, `unitsConsumed`, quote/build `summary`).
- An RPC-reported revert is a **typed `success:false` result** with `err`/`logs` — never a thrown error, never fabricated success. Transport/build failures throw typed errors exactly like the execute paths.
- Handlers opt in via `simulate()` + `simulation` capability metadata (Solana `swap` and `pump_fun_buy` today); every other handler/subaction combination returns a typed `SIMULATION_UNSUPPORTED` router failure — never a silent fallback to `execute()` or to the `prepare` echo.
- Simulation needs only the wallet **public key**: it never resolves a signing keypair or a `WalletBackend` signer (pump.fun simulate resolves `getAddresses().solana`, which works even for backends that cannot sign locally), and skips the pump.fun browser page-open side effect.
- `mode=simulate` skips the financial-confirmation gate (nothing signs or moves), documented in the gate's header, and the action's `mode` parameter docs state simulate cannot authorize or lead to broadcast.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature (non-breaking, additive change which adds functionality), resolving a fabricated-success defect.

# Documentation changes needed?

Done in this PR: `plugins/plugin-wallet/CLAUDE.md` (mode paragraph, handler extension recipe, pump.fun path), byte-identical `AGENTS.md`, and `README.md` mode summary. `bun run check:agents-claude` passes (153 pairs byte-identical).

Failure signatures: `simulateSolanaSwap is not a function` / `simulatePumpFunBuy is not a function` (5 tests); simulate dispatch → `INVALID_PARAMS` (mode not yet in the Zod enum); `SIMULATION_UNSUPPORTED` test → got `INVALID_PARAMS`; gate test → `true` instead of `false`; would-fail render test → parse failure. Restoring the implementation (byte-verified) → 26/26. The reviewing orchestrator independently re-ran all suites on the final tree.

Hard invariants proven by tests, not prose: `sendTransaction`/`sendRawTransaction`/`confirmTransaction` are spies that **throw** if touched during simulation; a `VersionedTransaction.prototype.sign` spy throws in the dedicated no-signing test; the existing fabrication-locking test for `prepare`/`dryRun` (`wallet-router.test.ts` "prepares dry-run metadata without executing") is untouched and green, proving those semantics did not change.

28:T16ec,Relates to #13115 (code path — issue remains open pending operator egress policy decision and cross-isolate hardening is now addressed via a Durable Object).

## Summary

Ground-up rework addressing all reviewer feedback from closed #18918 and CHANGES_REQUESTED from Uuriko on PR #19065.

## Changes

### Blocking fix #1 — Cross-isolate atomic quota enforcement

The original implementation relied on an isolate-local `Map` for in-flight reservations and a non-atomic KV read-modify-write for commits. Two Cloudflare isolates could both reserve against the same remaining budget and the last KV `put` would win, under-counting egress.

**Fix:** Added a new Durable Object `HfProxyEgressGate` (one actor per org) that owns the committed monthly egress total and in-flight reservations in its strongly-ordered storage. All `reserve`, `amend`, `commit`, and `release` operations are serialized inside the single-threaded actor, making cross-isolate lost updates impossible.

This follows the exact pattern of `INFERENCE_ADMISSION_GATES` and `ANONYMOUS_CHAT_GATES` — the established cloud architecture for per-org serialized state.

**New files:**
- `packages/cloud/api/src/hf-proxy-egress-gate.ts` — the DO class
- Binding added to `wrangler.toml` (binding + migration tag `hf-proxy-egress-gates-v1`)
- Type added to `cloud-worker-env.ts`
- Exported from `src/index.ts`

**Route changes:**
- `route.ts` now delegates `tryReserveEgress`, `amendReservation`, `commitReservation`, and a new `releaseReservation` to the DO when `HF_PROXY_EGRESS_GATES` is bound. The in-memory + KV path remains as a fallback for local dev/tests.
- All direct `inFlightReservations.delete()` calls in the route handler now go through `releaseReservation()`, which releases through the DO.
- Non-blocking fix: 4xx client errors (not just 401/403) now release the reservation.

### Blocking fix #2 — Title/body overclaim

- Changed `Fixes #13115` → `Relates to #13115` (the issue stays open)
- Corrected the title from "atomic" to "via Durable Object" — honest about the mechanism
- Updated the file header docblock to describe the DO-backed architecture and the fallback path clearly

### Non-blocking fix — Error response handling

Extended reservation release to all 4xx responses (not just 401/403), so a 404/429/etc. does not needlessly hold the budget.

## Tests

Added three new tests in the `Cross-isolate atomic egress quota (Durable Object path)` describe block:
1. **Sequential commits — no lost update**: Two isolates each stream 5 bytes against a 20-byte budget; the committed total must reach exactly 10, and a third request for 15 bytes must be rejected.
2. **Overlapping reservations — cross-isolate mutual exclusion**: Two concurrent requests against a 10-byte budget; exactly one succeeds and one is rejected before upstream fetch.
3. **Sequential exhaustion**: Four 5-byte downloads exhaust a 20-byte budget exactly; fifth request is 429.

The test fake simulates cross-isolate shared storage by having multiple stub instances (simulating separate isolates) read/write the same backing store — proving the state-sharing invariant the old KV path violated.

— [hermes/t3rpz]
29:T5871,# Fixes

Relates to https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/18045 (kept open: the deployed staging create → first-send proof remains tracked by #18045; this PR delivers the independently mergeable client slice) — the **client slice** completing the first-shared-agent-turn warming contract. The server/API slice (stable machine codes, `Retry-After: 1`, durable `clientMessageId` claim/replay, narrowed e2e classifier) merged in #19078; this PR covers the client-side items that comment left open: bounded warming absorption in the send path and the terminal `insufficient_credits` mapping.

Shipped here, per the issue's "Expected behavior":

- **The original send stays pending while the client absorbs only the structured warming responses.** The absorption lives at the request choke point (`ElizaClient.rawRequest`), so every browser consumer of the shared chat surfaces — messages REST, messages/stream, and the recovery/replay paths that route through it — gets the same behavior: a `503` whose body carries `code: agent_cache_warming` or `code: shared_runtime_cache_warming` waits the advertised `Retry-After` (clamped 250 ms–5 s) and re-issues the **byte-identical request**, so the same `clientMessageId` rides every retry and lands in #19078's idempotent claim/replay boundary. Budget: 4 re-issues **and** a ~5 s total-elapsed deadline across all warming waits (`WARMING_TOTAL_BUDGET_MS`) — an oversized `Retry-After` gets its wait clamped to the remaining budget instead of stretching to attempts × max-delay — matching the issue's "four attempts / roughly five seconds". The first warming wait also surfaces the existing `waking` turn status instead of stalled dots.

**Review response (changes-requested, 2026-08-13):** `rawRequest` is now ONE bounded classification loop — every response, first attempt or any retry, re-enters the same 401/202/warming classifier, so the states compose in any order: a 202 that follows an absorbed warming 503 runs the full resume contract (no placeholder "success"), a 401 mid-sequence still gets its one refresh, and the refreshed token is written back so every later re-issue (warming or resume) carries it. The 402 classification `useChatSend` consumes moved out of `/join` into a domain-neutral `packages/ui/src/api/credit-gate-error.ts`; `/join` keeps only the welcome-bonus interpretation as a wrapper over that canonical classifier.
- **The first non-warming response is surfaced immediately** — the loop exits on the first response that is not a named warming 503, success or failure.
- **A generic/unstructured 503 is not retried** (`inference_unavailable`, cache outage, code-less 503s stay real failures), **and a 402 is never retried**.
- **A terminal `code: insufficient_credits` JSON 402 now renders the existing out-of-credits turn** (banner + "Add credits" CTA, no Retry chip) instead of falling through `useChatSend` as a generic `provider_issue` Retry turn that re-hits the empty balance. The classifier is the same fail-closed 402 walk the /join surface uses (`describeJoinCreditGateError`), and the turn shows the server's own message.
- **E2E covering the warm-up never becoming user-visible failures:** a new real-browser e2e (`test:warming-absorption-e2e`) mounts the production `useChatSend` → `streamChatEndpoint` → `rawRequest` pipeline + ChatOverlay with the server simulated at the transport boundary, replays the issue's exact staging sequence (`agent_cache_warming` 503 → `shared_runtime_cache_warming` 503 → streamed reply), and asserts the send stays pending with no Retry chip and no error notice until the reply lands — then asserts the 402 renders terminally.

Reachability: this is the production first-shared-agent-chat path — the user-visible new-user QA flow the issue reproduces on staging. The absorption sits in the shared browser client, so the deployed runtime's named warming codes from #19078 are consumed by exactly this code.

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

# Risks

**Low.** The warming loop triggers only on a `503` whose parsed body carries one of the two named codes — a shape no other server response uses — and is bounded (4 re-issues), abort-aware (caller `AbortSignal` short-circuits both the wait and the re-issue), and skipped entirely for `allowNonOk` probes, so readiness/status polls keep their raw responses. Retrying is idempotent by server contract: both barriers reject **before** admission (#19078), and the re-issued body is byte-identical, so the durable `clientMessageId` claim de-dupes any request that did land. The 402 mapping only fires on the canonical `status === 402 && code === insufficient_credits` shape (fail-closed classifier); a code-less 402 keeps today's generic path (covered by a test).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

First shared-agent chat: instead of surfacing the two expected cache-warming 503s as user-visible failures before the first reply, the client absorbs them inside the original pending send and shows the first real response. A drained-balance 402 becomes the designed out-of-credits turn instead of a retry loop that cannot succeed.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Exact revision

- Base: `0c1593ad5fc8d52288d04626fb0f46d14743d1f7`
- Head: `afac817814707e95bc0a1bd1cbaa91c41a78dbe8`
- Tree: `f2cf826e1c1ada98f93f00824de1cb0bc5fad631`
- Scope: exactly 14 `packages/ui/**` paths; no homepage, backend, runtime, auth implementation, JoinPage, provider, staging, or deploy path.

# Risks

**Medium.** `/get-started` becomes a reviewed public, zero-network consent/pause boundary. It intentionally does not synchronize or refresh a stored Steward session until the user explicitly continues to private `/join`. The route renders no protected server data. Messaging-continuation completion remains paused because the current server endpoint couples linking to credit/compute mutation.

# What this changes

- Bare `/get-started` explains setup and requires an explicit click before `/join` can select/create an agent or start compute.
- Opaque messaging-continuation presence is retained locally and shown as paused without previewing, claiming, linking, granting credit, running inference, or provisioning compute.
- Malformed tokens fail visibly.
- Persistence is verified by exact write/readback. Either verified browser store permits URL sanitization; total failure preserves the URL copy and shows a focused error.
- Storage presence is a three-state authority: `present`, `absent`, or `indeterminate`. Setup consent appears only after both stores are conclusively absent.
- Retry re-runs the failed `persist`, `peek`, or `clear` phase. Dismiss reaches consent only through clear-and-verify of both stores.
- Router-owned replacement preserves unrelated query parameters, hash, and router state. StrictMode/remount does not repeat writes or renew expiry; same-route token arrival is handled.
- Only `/get-started` bypasses Steward session synchronization. `/join` remains private and unchanged.
- All production copy is localized in the existing eight Cloud catalogs.

This deliberately does **not** invent the missing account-scoped Shared transcript/quota/capability DTO, Dedicated quote/confirm/status DTO, or a non-mutating continuation-claim endpoint.

# Risks

Low. Cross-tab completion remains advisory. The waiting tab now accepts a recovered session only after the server-verified JWT email matches the email challenge it initiated. This recovery path never deletes a session. Login destinations pass through one parsed, same-origin sanitizer that rejects backslashes and cross-origin forms.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Publishes token-free cross-tab completion only after callback cookie synchronization succeeds.
- Recovers a waiting tab through bounded, abortable refresh polling without destructive session deletion.
- Binds recovered sessions to the challenged email, ignoring stale, absent, malformed, and wrong-account sessions.
- Uses one parsed same-origin return destination sanitizer across query, persisted, callback, and waiting-tab paths.
- Preserves explicit resend/back actions for expired, consumed, invalid, and incomplete callbacks.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is required; source comments and focused regression tests define the security contract.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/ui/src/cloud/public-pages/lib/steward-session.ts`, then `login-return-to.ts`, `steward-login-section.tsx`, and their focused tests.

## Detailed testing steps

- Hostile `/\\evil.example`, absolute external, and protocol-relative destinations fall back to `/join` through real callback and waiting-tab integration paths.
- A stale marker plus expired cookie performs bounded POST refresh attempts, never DELETE.
- A valid matching cookie arriving after two rejected refreshes succeeds on the third refresh.
- A valid JWT for another account is ignored until the challenged account's JWT arrives.

2d:T19c0,# Relates to

Relates to #18455 (non-closing). This patch hardens callback validation and readiness ordering, but it does not deliver the live Google authorization/callback, restart-persistence, revoke, read-only Gmail/Calendar, and browser evidence still required by that issue.

# Summary

- Validate `GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI` at the real connector `startOAuth` boundary and return no redirect URI for malformed or unreachable configurations.
- Carry the actual served origin through `ConnectorOAuthStartRequest`; the Settings route ignores spoofable forwarding headers and uses `ELIZA_EXTERNAL_BASE_URL` as the only reverse-proxy authority.
- Require exact scheme, canonical host, effective port, and callback path. Reject credentials, query strings, fragments, malformed hosts, fake `127.*` DNS names, and distinct loopback listeners.
- Share strict loopback classification between core, agent, Google Workspace, and LifeOps.
- Keep a persisted connected grant usable when callback configuration later breaks, while pending/error accounts retain actionable callback diagnostics.
- Infer chat/internal callback reachability only from configured external origin or the API bind/port; wildcard binds prove local reachability without pretending distinct loopback addresses are equivalent.

This is the narrowest deep implementation because the trust decision is enforced at both ingress and provider boundaries: the agent establishes an authoritative origin, and the Google provider independently refuses to create an OAuth URL when that origin cannot receive the callback.

# Risk

Existing invalid callback values that were previously accepted now fail early. Valid public HTTPS and exact loopback deployments remain supported. No UI, prompt, model, or database schema changes are included.

2e:T3d09,# Relates to

Relates to https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/18045 — this is the **API slice** of the first-shared-agent-turn warming contract. It does **not** close the issue; the client-side absorption work remains (listed under Known gaps).

Shipped here, per the issue's "Expected behavior":

- Both warming barriers now emit **stable machine codes** — `agent_cache_warming` (authorization-scope hydration, previously code-less) and `shared_runtime_cache_warming` — with **`Retry-After: 1`** on every shared chat surface: messages REST, messages/stream, bridge, canonical agent stream, and the shell catch-all. The conversation-stream path no longer loses the retry header while propagating the error.
- A caller-supplied **`clientMessageId` now enters a coordinator-owned durable claim/replay/conflict boundary** (per the exact-head review): the conversation Durable Object claims the key in transactional storage BEFORE any admission or provider dispatch and persists the terminal result BEFORE the response can leave the coordinator. A same-key retry (a lost response) **replays the stored result — no second admission, provider dispatch, reservation, or charge** — and a reused key with different text is rejected as a structured non-retryable `409 client_message_conflict` on every shared surface. Client-keyed turns also derive **deterministic billing identities** (`requestId`, `idempotencyKey`) from the key, so even a crash-and-retry re-execution replays one debit identity at the admission gate instead of opening a second charge. The id still becomes the bridge RPC id, so `turnMessageIds` stays stable too.
- The cache-outage 503 gets the distinct code `agent_cache_unavailable`, and the e2e warming classifier (`isSharedRuntimeWarming`) now matches **only** the two named warming codes — a generic retryable 503 (`inference_unavailable`, cache outage, missing Worker binding) is a real failure again, not warming.

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

- [x] This PR targets `develop` and is rebased onto the latest `origin/develop`
      with zero conflicts (`git fetch origin && git rebase origin/develop`) — two commits on `develop@9d54a097`.
- [x] Focused package gates were run after sync (transcripts below). Root `bun run verify` was not run on this machine (multi-hour matrix); recorded in **Known gaps** — hosted CI covers it.
- [x] A reviewer can confirm the change works without reading the code, from the
      evidence attached below.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto the latest `origin/develop` (`9d54a097`); zero conflicts.
- [x] `bun run verify` post-sync passed 350/350 tasks on the exact head.

# Risks

Low.

- The `Retry-After: 1` header is additive on responses that were already 503 + `retryable: true`; clients that ignore it are unaffected.
- `clientMessageId` behavior changes **only when the caller supplies one**; absent, each send still gets a fresh `crypto.randomUUID()` and fresh billing identities, byte-for-byte the old behavior. The new `409 client_message_conflict` fires only when a caller reuses a key with different text — which previously silently replaced the landed transcript pair.
- The claim ledger is one bounded value per conversation Durable Object (32 claims / 256 KB, oldest evicted). An evicted claim degrades to a fresh execution whose deterministic `requestId` still dedupes the charge at the admission gate — never a wedged room.
- The tightened e2e classifier only narrows what tests absorb as warming — it can surface real failures earlier, never hide them.
- The new `code` field on `ResolvedSharedAgent` errors is optional; existing consumers that ignore it are unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Names the two expected first-shared-turn warming barriers with stable machine codes, advertises `Retry-After: 1` on every shared chat surface (including the POST paths that previously omitted it), makes client-keyed shared turns durably idempotent at the conversation coordinator (claim before admission/dispatch, durable terminal-result replay, payload-conflict rejection, deterministic billing identities), and tightens the e2e warming classifier so generic 503s are no longer classified as warming.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The contract is documented in-code where the codes are emitted and classified.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/shared-runtime/resolve-shared-agent.ts` — the authorization-scope barrier now returns `code: "agent_cache_warming"` + `retryAfterSeconds: 1`, and the outage path returns `code: "agent_cache_unavailable"`.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/shared-runtime/shared-runtime-chat.ts` — the claim boundary (`SharedTurnClaimStore`, `claimSharedTurn`) runs before `admitTurn`; `admitTurn` derives deterministic `requestId`/`idempotencyKey` from the claim key; `complete` persists the terminal result after the durable history merge and before the response/done frame.
- `packages/cloud/api/src/shared-runtime-conversation.ts` — the Durable Object owns the bounded claim ledger in transactional storage and translates conflicts to a structured 409.
- `packages/cloud/api/__tests__/shared-agent-turn-idempotency.test.ts` — the real route → coordinator → Durable Object → chat service proof: cold create → first send → retry.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/shared-runtime/shared-rest-adapter.ts` and `canonical-scoped-stream.ts` — caller-supplied `clientMessageId` becomes the bridge RPC id and the `params.clientMessageId` claim marker.
- `packages/cloud/e2e/src/helpers/shared-runtime.ts` — `isSharedRuntimeWarming` matches only the two named codes; `shared-runtime.test.ts` beside it is the focused classifier suite.
- The five route files render the code + `Retry-After` header on their 503s.

## Detailed testing steps

Automated tests cover the contract:

- Warming 503s carry `Retry-After` + the named code on REST send, stream, bridge, canonical stream, and catch-all (route tests + adapter tests).
- **Durable idempotency (service boundary, `shared-runtime-chat.test.ts`):** two same-key bridge submissions produce ONE provider dispatch, ONE admission, ONE billed settlement, and byte-identical terminal results; a completed keyed stream turn replays its terminal frames without re-dispatch; same key + different text throws `SharedTurnConflictError` before admission; a failed keyed turn re-executes under the SAME `requestId`/`idempotencyKey`.
- **Durable idempotency (real route/coordinator boundary, `shared-agent-turn-idempotency.test.ts`):** through the real messages route → `coordinateSharedBridge` → real `SharedRuntimeConversation` Durable Object → real chat service (only provider dispatch and money seams are counted doubles): cold create → first send answers the documented warming 503 → warm retry executes once → same-key retry replays the identical body with dispatch/admission/charge counters still at 1 → different-text reuse is a structured 409 → a REBUILT Durable Object over the same storage still replays instead of re-dispatching, and the single admission carries the deterministic client-keyed `idempotencyKey`.
- A supplied `clientMessageId` is used as the RPC id + claim marker on REST send and canonical stream; absent id still generates a fresh UUID and never carries the marker.
- `isSharedRuntimeWarming` (focused suite `packages/cloud/e2e/src/helpers/shared-runtime.test.ts`, run with `bun test ./packages/cloud/e2e/src/helpers/shared-runtime.test.ts`) accepts exactly the two named codes and rejects `retryable: true` 503s with foreign or missing codes (`inference_unavailable`, `agent_cache_unavailable`, `shared_runtime_context_unavailable`), non-503s, and non-retryable envelopes.

## Known gaps / failures

- **This PR does not close #18045.** Remaining client-side work from the issue's expected behavior: (1) `useChatSend` keeping the original send pending while automatically absorbing **only** the two named warming codes within a bounded budget (~4 attempts / ~5s) honoring `Retry-After`; (2) mapping a terminal `code: insufficient_credits` 402 to the existing out-of-credits state/CTA instead of a generic `provider_issue` retry turn; (3) the shared create → first-send E2E proving the warm-up states never become two user-visible failures. This PR supplies the stable codes, headers, and idempotent-retry substrate that work needs.
- Root `bun run verify` passed 350/350 tasks on the exact head.
- No staging deploy evidence: this laptop has no staging deploy/tail access. The status/code sequence on staging was already captured in the issue body (2026-08-08). The credential-isolated **live** staging create → cold first send → retry artifact requested on #18426 therefore still needs a maintainer/operator credential — the same blocker recorded there (staging deploys only from `develop`; contributor-side credentials fail the isolation preflight). What this PR supplies instead is the in-repo real-boundary equivalent: `shared-agent-turn-idempotency.test.ts` drives the actual route → conversation coordinator → `SharedRuntimeConversation` Durable Object → chat service path (cold create → first send → retry → replay-after-restart) with dispatch/admission/charge counters, at the exact head above.
- **Duplicate disposition:** #18426 was the prior, closed attempt at this same #18045 contract; it was closed with instructions to re-land only after durable, authority-safe idempotency with exact integration coverage. This PR is that re-land: the durable claim/replay/conflict boundary is coordinator-owned (Durable Object transactional storage), billing identities are deterministic per claim key (the admission gate's `requestId`-keyed pending-charge/debit-replay makes re-execution charge-idempotent), and the integration coverage above exercises the real boundary at the exact head.

# Risks

Medium. This tightens Google OAuth grant handling and required-capability checks. Existing accounts with incomplete `grantedCapabilities` metadata may need to reconnect before narrower Google Workspace operations can run.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Hardens Google Workspace OAuth scope handling so the connector stays least-privilege:

- Sends `include_granted_scopes=false` for Google OAuth starts.
- Rejects malformed, blank, duplicate, and semantically duplicate requested scope inputs.
- Stores the requested capabilities on the OAuth flow and rejects supported Google capabilities returned by the provider when they were not requested.
- Keeps unknown legacy provider scopes out of accepted capabilities while preserving token material.
- Requires stored connector accounts to include the capability needed by the Google Workspace operation before resolving credentials.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Risks

The change is isolated to core model routing and evaluator input budgeting. It adds an internal per-attempt preparation hook so the budget is derived from the provider registration that the runtime actually selected, including owner overrides and same-model-type failovers. Unknown model metadata retains the conservative 128k fallback and is identified as unresolved in terminal diagnostics.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Resolves and applies the evaluator context budget for every actual provider attempt instead of pre-compacting all input to the smallest reachable fallback.
- Preserves large-context input byte-for-byte until a smaller selected attempt requires compaction.
- Recomputes cache metadata and keeps evaluator thinking disabled after every rerender.
- Guards structured tool-call parameters and rejects a still-over-budget attempt before its provider handler runs.
- Records a typed `EVALUATOR_INPUT_OVER_BUDGET` evaluation stage without falsely recording a provider failure.
- Keeps custom sub-10k context windows usable by capping the output reserve relative to the model window.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix.

# Documentation changes needed?

No documentation changes are needed; this is an internal runtime contract fix.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with `packages/core/src/runtime/evaluator.ts`, the per-attempt hook in `packages/core/src/runtime.ts`, and `packages/core/src/runtime/__tests__/evaluator-input-budget.test.ts`.

# Risks

**Medium, deliberate.** With no durable writer available at all (a runtime where the store service failed to register AND no vault service exists), connector OAuth completion now fails closed with an actionable error instead of silently recording a connected account whose token dies at the next restart. Nothing loses the ability to connect: the store service now registers **unconditionally** — host vault when a durable bridge exists, state-dir PGlite vault otherwise — so hostless/standalone/Cloud boots gain a durable writer rather than losing the volatile one. On a headless host with neither OS keychain nor `ELIZA_VAULT_PASSPHRASE`, the vault write fails with `MasterKeyUnavailableError` (fail closed, actionable) rather than storing tokens that vanish on restart. Affected surfaces: OAuth credential persistence in plugin-google-workspace, plugin-github, plugin-slack, plugin-x, and plugin-calendar (Microsoft).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Installs a durable connector credential writer on standalone/Cloud boot, using the durable pieces already in-repo, then deletes the volatile SECRETS writer from every connector's credential persistence path:

- `ConnectorCredentialStoreService` (`packages/agent/src/services/connector-credential-store.ts`) now registers **unconditionally** (`packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza.ts`). It is backed by the host vault (`AgentHostBridge.sharedVault()`) when an embedding host installed a durable bridge, and otherwise by a state-dir PGlite vault (`createVault()` from `@elizaos/vault`, rooted at `ELIZA_STATE_DIR`) that the service opens itself. One vault per process (PGlite is single-writer). No topology flags — no `ELIZA_CLOUD_PROVISIONED` read, no `ELIZA_REQUIRE_DURABLE_CONNECTOR_CREDENTIALS`, no `volatile` opt-in, no per-plugin policy.
- The `SECRETS` writer is removed from `resolveVaultWriters` in plugin-google-workspace, plugin-github, plugin-slack, and plugin-x, and from the Microsoft secret-backend/writer candidates in plugin-calendar — all five divergent copies in this same PR. `SECRETS` remains a **read-side** probe for refs written by older builds. With no durable writer available, `persistConnectorCredentialRefs` fails closed unconditionally: no ref recorded, no account marked connected.
- `ConnectorAccountManager.completeOAuth` (`packages/core/src/connectors/account-manager.ts`) now persists a **terminal `failed` flow** when provider completion throws after the one-time state was consumed (review 4932167167 P1). The catch is scoped to the provider-completion call only — `upsertAccount` and the success-path `updateOAuthFlow` stay outside it, so a later account/success-state storage failure cannot relabel an already-connected account. The rethrow is a typed `ElizaError` (`code: CONNECTOR_OAUTH_COMPLETION_FAILED`, provider/flowId context, original failure on `cause`). Per review 4932872821 P1, the failed-state write is now required to return a **non-null** failed record: a `null` result and a throwing write are both treated as persistence failure, wrapped with the provider failure in one typed `ElizaError` (`code: CONNECTOR_OAUTH_FAILURE_STATE_PERSISTENCE_FAILED`, provider/flowId context) whose `cause` is the `AggregateError` — a bare `AggregateError` is never the thrown type. The **persisted public flow message is generic** ("start the flow again"); raw provider exception text stays on the thrown error's cause chain, never in the stored row. The `eliza.ts` store-start warning no longer claims a non-durable fallback — writes fail closed until the store is restored.
- **Cloud durability contract (review 4932872821 P1):** `DockerSandboxProvider` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/docker-sandbox-provider.ts`) now injects `ELIZA_STATE_DIR=/root/.eliza` — the `${volumePath}/eliza` bind mount, the same mount `PGLITE_DATA_DIR` and the pre-seeded `eliza.json` already live on — so the state-dir PGlite vault lands on the persistent agent volume instead of `/root/.local/state/eliza` in the container's writable layer. The per-launch `crypto.randomUUID()` `ELIZA_VAULT_PASSPHRASE` is replaced with a **stable per-agent master key**: read-or-created once at `${volumePath}/.vault-passphrase` (0600, 64 hex chars from `/dev/urandom` generated **on the node** — never derived from agent/org identifiers, never transiting a shell argument), so a replacement container over the same volume derives the same key and decrypts the vault it inherits. A caller-supplied `ELIZA_VAULT_PASSPHRASE` or `ELIZA_STATE_DIR` still wins; the read-or-create **fails closed** on an unusable persisted key rather than minting a fresh per-launch key. This covers existing (claimed) agent rows with no control-plane migration — the key lives with the volume the replacement flow already reattaches.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The behavior contract lives in the file headers of the touched modules and the regression tests.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/agent/test/connector-credential-durable.integration.test.ts` — the write → **real OS process restart** → read e2e over the real standalone entrypoint. Each phase spawns `startElizaProcess` (the same boot `bin.js start` runs) as a separate `bun` process sharing only a temp `ELIZA_STATE_DIR`; the restart is a genuinely new OS process. Runtime, plugin resolution, PGlite-backed plugin-sql adapter, the state-dir PGlite vault, and SQL ref storage are all real; nothing on the persistence path is stubbed and no Google HTTP endpoint is touched (no token-endpoint override of any kind).

The child fixture reads the token to persist through `runtime.getSetting("DURABLE_TOKEN_VALUE")` — never `process.env` — and fails closed if the setting is absent. The parent seeds it into the state dir's `eliza.json` agent settings, the same channel as the Google client config, because `runtime.getSetting` deliberately never reads `process.env`. Spawn-env `GOOGLE_CLIENT_*` remains only for plugin selection (`shouldLoadGoogleWorkspace`), a documented collector seam.

Then `plugins/plugin-google-workspace/src/connector-account-provider.writer-reject.test.ts` — the review-4932167167 P1 regression at the real manager→provider boundary: real `ConnectorAccountManager`, real Google provider, real `InMemoryDatabaseAdapter`; only the Google token-endpoint HTTP response is stubbed (successful exchange, so the failure under test is the writer). The registered durable store's `putSecret()` rejects; the first `completeOAuth` surfaces the typed error (`CONNECTOR_OAUTH_COMPLETION_FAILED`; the raw writer message rides `cause`, and the public message is generic) and the stored flow is terminal `failed` with the generic public message — not `pending`/errorless, and not raw provider text; the second completion with the same state hits the consumed-state guard. The two new review-4932872821 regressions live next to `completeOAuth` in `packages/core/src/connectors/account-manager.test.ts`: a throwing failed-state write and a null-returning one both produce the typed persistence-failure `ElizaError` (AggregateError as cause only).

Then `plugins/plugin-google-workspace/src/connector-credential-refs.test.ts` — the fail-closed regression: with only the in-memory SECRETS service present, persistence rejects, no token material lands in SECRETS, and no ref is recorded.

  test/calendar-ics-source.pglite.test.ts test/calendar-action-effect-receipts.pglite.test.ts
```

32:T96c,## Summary

Closes the remaining cold anonymous `/login` transfer cost for **#18056** after progressive public registration (#18441).

The app Vite config aliases bare `@elizaos/core` to a **non–tree-shakeable** prebuilt browser blob (~2.4 MB raw / ~0.66 MB gzip). Login-path UI and `@elizaos/shared` re-exports were still value-importing that entry, so the blob rode the cold `/login` graph as `eliza-globals` / `index.browser`.

### Changes

- Expand `@elizaos/core/client-public` pure surface (surface manifests, shortcuts, activity plaintext, swarm activity, connectors, env-alias reader, …).
- Migrate remaining `packages/ui` bare-core **value** imports to `client-public` / `@elizaos/logger`.
- Stop `@elizaos/shared` from re-exporting bare core for:
  - `settings-debug` (local pure copy)
  - HTTP helpers (browser facade; Node should use `@elizaos/core` for real body readers)
  - connectors / connector-config checks (`client-public`)
  - `resolveAliasedEnvValue` / `isTruthyEnvValue` / `formatError` (local pure copies so `vite.config` can load shared without package subpath resolution)
- Vite: optional `ELIZA_18056_CORE_STUB=1` points bare core at `client-public` for residual-edge diagnosis.

## Measurement (local harness)

Conditions: production `bun --bun vite build` for `packages/app`, static gzip server, Playwright Chromium, `serviceWorkers: block`, settle 6s, `/login` only.

| Build | Script transfer (desktop) | Dominant cold scripts |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| develop / prior head | **~1.67 MB** | `eliza-globals` / `index.browser` ~0.66 MB + main/i18n/state/voice |
| this branch | **~1.03 MB** | i18n ~0.12, main ~0.11, state ~0.08, voice ~0.07 — **no core blob** |

# Risks

Low for durable-host deployments (behavior unchanged: the
`connector_credential_store` service remains the first writer). The #18080
contract is now split by topology (decision recorded on #18080):

- **Durability-expected runtimes fail closed**: Cloud-provisioned containers
  (`ELIZA_CLOUD_PROVISIONED=1`), runtimes where a durable store/vault service
  registered but failed to start, and operators who set
  `ELIZA_REQUIRE_DURABLE_CONNECTOR_CREDENTIALS=1`. OAuth completion rejects
  with a typed `ElizaError` (`CONNECTOR_CREDENTIAL_WRITER_UNAVAILABLE`), the
  flow is persisted as terminally failed with that code, and no secret or
  credential ref is recorded.
- **Hostless local desktop/dev keeps its pre-existing behavior**: the volatile
  SECRETS keep-until-restart write still connects the account, now explicitly
  flagged `credentialRefStorage.volatile` in account metadata and warned in
  the log, instead of silently masquerading as durable.

Sibling connectors (slack, github, x, microsoft calendar) still carry their
own diverged copies of the volatile SECRETS fallback and are intentionally out
of scope here — flagged as follow-up in #18080.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Hardens `persistConnectorCredentialRefs`
(`plugins/plugin-google-workspace/src/connector-credential-refs.ts`) and the
core OAuth completion path against the #18080 dangling-vaultRef window.
`SECRETS` global storage mutates `runtime.character.settings.secrets` in
process memory only
(`packages/core/src/features/secrets/storage/character-store.ts`), so treating
it as a durable writer marked an OAuth account connected while its credential
died with the process and the persisted `vaultRef` dangled forever after.

After this change:

1. **Durability-expected topologies fail closed with a typed error.** On a
   Cloud-provisioned container, when a registered durable store failed to
   start, or under the explicit
   `ELIZA_REQUIRE_DURABLE_CONNECTOR_CREDENTIALS=1` opt-in,
   `persistConnectorCredentialRefs` throws an `ElizaError` with stable code
   `CONNECTOR_CREDENTIAL_WRITER_UNAVAILABLE`, fatal severity, and structured
   non-secret context (provider, accountId, probed service names, which
   topology signal triggered fail-closed). The account stays `pending`; no
   secret or credential ref is recorded.
2. **Failed OAuth completion is terminally recorded, not stuck pending.**
   `ConnectorAccountManager.completeOAuth` (packages/core) now persists an
   explicit `status: "failed"` flow — preserving the provider error text and,
   for `ElizaError`s, the stable code in flow metadata — when the provider
   throws after the one-time state is consumed. If the failure-status write
   itself fails, both failures are preserved via `AggregateError`. The
   connector callback route serializes the `ElizaError` code and context in
   its structured 400 response, so callers can classify the failure instead of
   parsing prose.
3. **Hostless local desktop/dev keeps keep-until-restart.** Without any
   durability expectation, the volatile SECRETS write proceeds as it always
   has, now flagged `volatile: true` in the persist result and in
   `credentialRefStorage` account metadata, with a log warning naming the
   restart window and the two remediations (install a durable host vault, or
   opt into fail-closed).
4. **Reads unchanged.** `SECRETS` remains a read-side probe in
   `credential-resolver.ts`, so refs written before this hardening still
   resolve within the same process.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking for durable hosts; deliberate fail-closed change for
durability-expected hostless topologies per the product decision recorded on
#18080; hostless desktop/dev behavior preserved).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. The file
headers of the touched modules document the new contract.

37:T19df,Closes #18056.

## Root cause

The document entry imported `src/main.tsx` before route selection. Anonymous hosted routes therefore evaluated the complete cross-platform application composition root—including native bridges, plugin/runtime surfaces, and agent-dashboard dependencies—even though `/login` only renders the public Cloud shell. The earlier #18441 work added measurement and chunking but left that dominant eager edge intact.

## What changed

- add a tiny entry selector that chooses a dedicated hosted public/auth renderer before importing the full application
- preserve the existing full boot for desktop, native, disabled-web-shell, chat-harness, app-host `/`, and every non-public route
- reload once when public-shell navigation crosses into a full-app route so the selector reevaluates the new path
- directly import `AppProvider` from its owning module to avoid a cross-chunk re-export cycle
- calibrate an enforced cold `/login` budget from the working renderer
- make the transfer harness fail closed on console/page errors so a crashed, smaller page cannot pass the performance gate

38:T1258,## What

`on_mention` mode now allows an unaddressed follow-up only when the same sender owns a recent, connector-confirmed visible reply in that room.

The continuity anchor is runtime-local, receipt-driven, bounded, and fail-closed:

- A resolved callback or empty receipt array is not delivery proof.
- Only nonempty matching agent/entity/room `Memory` receipts with transcript-visible reply content qualify.
- Provider receipt timestamps—not callback completion order or inbound event time—order ownership.
- Older receipts cannot overwrite newer ones; equal-time cross-sender receipts invalidate the room.
- Pending same-room deliveries are drained before the ingress decision, including deliveries registered while an earlier barrier is being awaited.
- Anchors expire after five minutes, reject future clocks, cap at 256 rooms per runtime, and never write persistent cache state.
- Internal/action-result/blank/IGNORE/STOP rows, rejected callbacks, other reply-gate modes, and agent-self turns never open continuity.

# Risks

Low. One public read-only endpoint. Page 0 responses are unchanged except `total`/`hasMore` becoming correct; pages past the first go from always-empty to populated. The per-source fetch window is pinned to the repositories' existing 200-row clamp, which was already the hard ceiling on what discovery could see, so database load per request is bounded exactly as before.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`GET /api/v1/discovery` applied the pagination offset twice: each source's `listPublic` received `{ limit, offset }` (SQL `LIMIT ... OFFSET ...`) and the handler then sliced `[offset, offset + limit)` again over the merged/filtered/deduped/sorted set. Any request with `offset >= limit` returned an empty `services` array and `total` reported one already-truncated page, so clients paginating by offset stopped after page 1. Sources are now fetched from row 0 with the widest window their repositories allow, and pagination is applied exactly once after the merge; `total` is the full post-filter match count.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. (The window constant documents the repository clamp inline.)

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

The `sourceWindow` comment block in `packages/cloud/api/v1/discovery/route.ts`, then `__tests__/discovery-pagination-single-offset.test.ts` (real route + real repositories on in-process PGlite, 120 seeded public characters, no service mocks).

## Detailed testing steps

```bash
cd packages/cloud/api && bun test __tests__/discovery-pagination-single-offset.test.ts
```

3a:T1ac5,## What

Adds a **Cartesia** batch lane to `/api/v1/voice/stt` using Cartesia's prerecorded `/stt` endpoint (`ink-whisper`), selected with `VOICE_BATCH_STT_PROVIDER=cartesia`.

`VOICE_BATCH_STT_PROVIDER` pins `deepgram` | `cartesia` | `whisper` | `elevenlabs`; a pinned provider whose binding is missing **fails closed (503)** rather than silently degrading. Un-pinned selection is unchanged: free Whisper stays the default wherever `WHISPER_STT_URL` is set.

## Review change: the key-presence default was removed

The lane originally made Cartesia the un-pinned default whenever `CARTESIA_API_KEY` was present, by analogy with TTS. That was reverted on review (`4267c45`), for two reasons:

1. **It contradicts the rule stated three lines above the new branch.** The route already says: *"Deepgram prerecorded is opt-in because the same key also powers realtime Flux sessions. Key presence alone must not silently move every paid batch transcription onto Deepgram in production without an explicit rollout."* `CARTESIA_API_KEY` is in exactly that position — it powers the realtime Ink sessions (#17869 / #17997) — and it is *already set* on `eliza-cloud-api-prod` and `-staging`, so the default would have taken effect on deploy with no rollout step.
2. **The TTS analogy does not carry.** TTS has no free lane; batch STT does. `WHISPER_STT_URL` is set on the live worker, so the flip would have converted working zero-credit transcription into 402s for every such user — a silent free→paid change whose only mitigation was an operator noticing and setting the variable back.

Everything else in the lane is unchanged: `reserve → dispatch → bill/refund`, the `elevenlabs/scribe_v1` pricing proxy, fail-closed **502 + refund** on any upstream/parse failure with no cross-provider fallback, `X-API-Key` auth, transcript/filename log redaction, seconds→ms word-span mapping, and BCP-47 → ISO-639-1 language normalization.

Batch REST (`POST https://api.cartesia.ai/stt`, multipart) is used rather than a one-shot wrapper around the realtime Ink WS — the WS only accepts raw 16 kHz mono PCM in 100 ms frames, which a Worker cannot transcode container formats to. Batch REST accepts exactly the container formats the route's magic-number allowlist already admits.

The realtime path is untouched: this PR only modifies the batch route, its types, and its tests.

## Tests

`bun test packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/stt/route.test.ts` → **65 pass / 1 skip (live gate) / 0 fail**, covering the full override matrix, fail-closed-on-missing-binding for every pin, the wire contract (headers/model/multipart/language-normalization/exact bytes), reserve→bill→usage, 402-before-call, word-span mapping, and malformed/5xx/transport → 502 + refund + no-secret-logs. The key-presence case is now asserted in the opposite direction: a configured Cartesia key alone must leave batch STT on free Whisper, with no reservation and no billing. Targeted Biome clean.

## Deploy notes

- No behavior change on deploy. Cartesia batch STT activates only when an operator sets `VOICE_BATCH_STT_PROVIDER=cartesia`; worth one canary STT request when they do.
- `Cartesia-Version: 2026-03-01` (the documented version for `/stt`).
- Cartesia batch returns word timestamps only — responses have `words`, never `segments` (downstream must tolerate absence, as it already must for the ElevenLabs lane).
- `CARTESIA_API_KEY` is already set on `eliza-cloud-api-prod` + `-staging`; no new secret needed.

- Agent tooling: `claude-code`
